# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Course::addSession
Summary: Adds a new session to the course on a specific date.
Algorithm: Check if a session already exists for the given date. If not, create a new Session object, add to the sessions list, and return the updated list.
Related Features: sessions
Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
Input: date: LocalDate (The date of the session in yyyy-MM-dd format)
Output: List<Session> (The updated list of sessions for the course)
Pre-condition: No other session exists for the course on the same date.
Post-condition: A new session is added to the sessions list.

Course::addDocument
Summary: Adds a new document to the course's collection of referenced documents.
Algorithm: Check if document is null or already exists in the list. If not, add to the list and return true.
Related Features: documents
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc: Document (The document object to be added)
Output: boolean (True if added successfully, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: doc must not be null.
Post-condition: The document is added to the course's document list.

Course::cancelSession
Summary: Cancels a session for the course by its date.
Algorithm: Find the session by date. If found and the session has no participants, remove it from the list and return true. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: sessions
Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
Input: date: LocalDate (The date of the session to cancel)
Output: boolean (True if removed successfully, false if session has participants or does not exist)
Pre-condition: The session exists and has an empty list of registered participants.
Post-condition: The specified session is removed from the list.

Course::removeDocument
Summary: Removes a document from the course's referenced documents.
Algorithm: Locate the document in the list. If found, remove it and return true. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: documents
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc: Document (The document to remove)
Output: boolean (True if removed, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: doc must exist in the course's document list.
Post-condition: The document is removed from the course's document list.

# Operation Specifications
School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new course to the school's catalog.
Algorithm: Verify if a course with the given unique ID already exists. If not, create a new Course instance, add it to the courses list, and return true.
Related Features: courses
Related Classes: Course
Input: id: String (Case-sensitive unique identifier for the course)
Output: boolean (True if added, false if ID already exists)
Pre-condition: id must not be null or empty.
Post-condition: A new Course object is added to the school's catalog.

School::addSector
Summary: Adds a new sector to the school.
Algorithm: Create a new Sector instance with the given ID and add it to the sectors list.
Related Features: sectors
Related Classes: Sector
Input: id: String (Unique identifier for the sector)
Output: Sector (The newly created sector object)
Pre-condition: id must be unique among existing sectors.
Post-condition: A new sector is added to the school.

School::assignCourseToSector
Summary: Assigns a course to a specific sector.
Algorithm: Find the course and the target sector by their IDs. If both exist, call currentSector.removeCourse(course), then targetSector.addCourse(course), update course reference, and return true.
Related Features: courses, sectors
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input: courseId: String, sectorId: String
Output: boolean (True if assignment successful)
Pre-condition: Course and Sector exist.
Post-condition: The course is associated with the new sector and removed from the previous one.

{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@432af457 (name: assignCourseToSector) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Assigns a course to a specific sector, ensuring it belongs to exactly one sector at a time.
Algorithm: 
1. Find the Course object with courseId and the Sector object with sectorId.
2. If either is not found, return false.
3. If the course currently has a sector assigned, call currentSector.removeCourse(course).
4. Call targetSector.addCourse(course).
5. Update the course.sector reference to point to targetSector.
6. Return true.
Related Features: courses, sectors
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input: courseId: String, sectorId: String
Output: boolean (True if assignment successful, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: The course and the sector exist in the system.
Post-condition: The course is associated with the new sector and removed from the previous one (if any).}

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions

Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Session::assignTrainer
Summary: Assigns a trainer to the session.
Algorithm: Check if current date is before the session date. If yes, update the trainer attribute and return true; else return false.
Related Features: trainer, date
Related Classes: Trainer, LocalDate
Input: t: Trainer, today: LocalDate
Output: boolean (True if assigned, false otherwise)
Pre-condition: today < date.
Post-condition: The session has the specified trainer assigned.

Session::registerParticipant
Summary: Registers a participant to the session.
Algorithm: Check if participant is already in registeredParticipants. If not, add to the list and return true; else return false.
Related Features: registeredParticipants
Related Classes: Participant, LocalDate
Input: p: Participant, today: LocalDate
Output: boolean (True if registered)
Pre-condition: Participant is not already registered.
Post-condition: Participant is added to the session's participant list.

Session::replaceTrainer
Summary: Replaces the current trainer of the session with a new one.
Algorithm: Check if current date is before the session date. If yes, update trainer and return true; else return false.
Related Features: trainer, date
Related Classes: Trainer, LocalDate
Input: newTrainer: Trainer, today: LocalDate
Output: boolean (True if replaced)
Pre-condition: today < date.
Post-condition: The session trainer is updated.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

  3.3 You should not change the method signature;

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public void eUnset(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case TrainingSchoolPackage.SCHOOL__NAME:
        setName(NAME_EDEFAULT);
        return;
      case TrainingSchoolPackage.SCHOOL__COURSES:
        getCourses().clear();
        return;
      case TrainingSchoolPackage.SCHOOL__SECTORS:
        getSectors().clear();
        return;
    }
    super.eUnset(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public boolean eIsSet(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case TrainingSchoolPackage.SCHOOL__NAME:
        return NAME_EDEFAULT == null ? name != null : !NAME_EDEFAULT.equals(name);
      case TrainingSchoolPackage.SCHOOL__COURSES:
        return courses != null && !courses.isEmpty();
      case TrainingSchoolPackage.SCHOOL__SECTORS:
        return sectors != null && !sectors.isEmpty();
    }
    return super.eIsSet(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString()
  {
    if (eIsProxy()) return super.toString();

    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder(super.toString());
    result.append(" (name: ");
    result.append(name);
    result.append(')');
    return result.toString();
  }

} // School

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
